## 2026-10-07 — approved YooKassa test checkout for club access

### Objective and approval boundary

The user approved one-off test club payment followed by access, without saving
cards or automatic renewals. Shop payments, real-money payments, refunds and
recurring YooKassa subscriptions are outside this stage. The user approved this
plan and the additive checkout-intent table on 2026-10-07. Enabling the test stand
remains a separate checkpoint. Existing incomplete project tasks are retained.

### Contract and architecture

- Keep Prodamus unchanged while the test adapter is disabled. No fallback to a
  live provider on error. Add a client-generated UUID intent key in the HTTP
  `Idempotency-Key` header (not the tRPC input),
  required only in YooKassa test mode. Preserve `{url, user: null}` and existing
  actor-email/blacklist checks; checkout never authenticates or verifies email.
- Claim the key atomically in a proposed `billing_checkout_intents` table with
  unique intent/order/provider-payment bindings. Persist recipient, test shop,
  price, access period, immutable request hash/payload, first dispatch time and
  processing state before calling out. Same key + changed body is a conflict.
  Concurrent requests cannot dispatch independent payments. No card data, raw
  provider dump or secrets in logs/Git; confirmation links are sensitive.
- Use fixed YooKassa HTTPS origin and validated IDs, no redirects, bounded time,
  response bytes and concurrency. Validate the account and each payment, requiring
  the configured shop and `test: true`; reject live keys. Create a one-stage
  redirect payment with `capture: true`, without a saved payment method. Persist
  provider ID before returning the validated URL. Metadata contains an opaque
  order ID, not email or authority to grant access.
- Network errors/HTTP 500 mean unknown outcome. Recover with the exact stored
  request/key only within a conservative window shorter than YooKassa's 24-hour
  idempotence guarantee. Never re-dispatch an unresolved older intent; reconcile.
  If provider ID is known, recover by GET, not by creating another payment.
- A webhook is a hint for an authenticated GET, not proof. Check ID, shop, test,
  amount, RUB, persisted order binding, `succeeded` and `paid`. Do not acknowledge
  transient verification/DB failures as successful processing. Handle webhook
  arrival before the create response is persisted, without issuing unbound access.
- Reuse atomic receipt + entitlement + outbox settlement with a test-specific
  provider namespace/order prefix. Browser return never grants access. A duplicate
  webhook never extends it again. Persist 30-day/180-day duration with the intent;
  do not derive it from mutable configuration. Old Prodamus cancellation/profile
  logic must not process YooKassa one-off orders. UI must label a one-off test
  purchase, not promise automatic renewals.
- Preserve API isolation: separately design bounded payment-only egress before
  rollout. Retain DB/SMTP/video/image services and old live-payment blocks.
  Configure HTTP notifications in the test shop's cabinet, not via Basic-auth API.

### Ordered slices, acceptance and commands

1. Y1 — HTTP/configuration boundary under `apps/backend/src/billing/yookassa/`
   and focused Vitest tests (2–4 files). Reject wrong shop/live responses and
   unsafe URLs; bound reads/time, redact errors. Test first with boundary-only
   transport doubles; no network in unit tests. No new SDK/dependencies.
2. Y2 — additive SQL/repository and disposable-MySQL tests (3–5 files), after Y1.
   Prove conflicts, concurrent retries, restart recovery and refusal of expired
   unknown outcomes. Schema approval precedes edits; migration rehearsal and
   preflight precede target DB changes. Never rewrite historical migrations.
3. Y3 — Nest checkout/webhook wiring and settlement (split into 3–5-file slices),
   after Y2. Test forgery, mismatched shop/amount/currency/test flag, blocked
   recipients, early webhook, transaction rollback and replay. Characterize
   existing Prodamus behavior. Checkpoint: review before caller/rollout work.
4. Y4 — caller contract handoff, after Y3. Retain the same intent through retry
   and reload, including timeout; do not create a key per attempt. Frontend edits
   require their own agreed scope, generated contract and browser checks.
5. Y5 — separately approved staging rollout, after Y4: protected backup,
   rehearsed migration, scoped egress/edge changes, cabinet notifications and
   exact image rollback. Real sandbox acceptance must prove payment → access,
   eventual access email and duplicate replay without extra receipt/outbox/period.

Use Node 22, committed pnpm 9.12.0 lockfile, existing Nest/Drizzle boundaries,
strict TypeScript and Zod. Example: `{ email, plan }` plus `Idempotency-Key: <UUID>` enters checkout;
`{ url, user: null }` leaves it. Recipient/price remain server-owned.

Run `pnpm exec vitest run tests/yookassa-*.test.ts` per slice, then `pnpm check`,
`pnpm build`, `pnpm check:api`, `pnpm check:boundaries` and `pnpm check:docs`.
Run SQL tests only under the repository's disposable `backend_test` policy.
Mocked tests are not SQL/provider acceptance. Run native `pnpm audit`, triage
reachable critical/high issues before release; never force dependency upgrades.
Build/test frontend only if changed. Do not reset staging data or claim production
readiness. Migration-history discrepancies remain a deployment gate.

References checked 2026-10-07: [request format and 24-hour idempotence](https://yookassa.ru/developers/using-api/interaction-format),
[payment lifecycle](https://yookassa.ru/developers/payment-acceptance/getting-started/payment-process),
[webhook verification and cabinet setup](https://yookassa.ru/developers/using-api/webhooks).

Implementation refinement: use the standard HTTP header so generated frontend

### Approved staging baseline transition — 2026-10-07

The user approved a protected backup and restore rehearsal before updating the
existing test stand. The observed database is `daryadarcy_staging`, 28 InnoDB
tables, without `__drizzle_migrations`; the initial canonical-schema baseline
already includes the changes in historical migrations 0017–0025. Replaying those
files or inserting fictional historical execution records is forbidden.

This is a narrow operator workflow for this one baseline, not a replacement for
production migration history. Keep the generic Drizzle preflight and historical
SQL bytes unchanged. A separately named stage-only upgrader will validate the
database name, full normalized SHOW CREATE TABLE fingerprint, exact 0026 SQL
SHA-256, and absence of views/triggers/routines/events. It permits only creation
of `billing_checkout_intents`; any unrelated schema drift fails closed. Serialize
operators with a MySQL advisory lock. MySQL DDL autocommits: on a lost response,
inspect the exact resulting schema on retry, never DROP/recreate an existing table.
The operator saves an actual-time release receipt outside the DB; no invented
Drizzle rows and no acceptance of arbitrary SQL, schema hashes or bypass flags.

Order and acceptance:

1. B1: owner-only config copies and consistent SQL dump, then restore into a
   separate memory/CPU-bounded MySQL container with no published ports/application
   workers. Compare every table's normalized DDL, counts and row digests inside
   the private server boundary; publish only aggregate verification. Preserve the
   protected backup, working volumes/services and rollback image IDs.
2. B2: tooling/operations stage-only policy + CLI and tests (at most four files).
   Test wrong DB/schema/SQL, unexpected executable objects, existing exact table,
   lock contention and DDL failure. Rehearse against the restored copy, proving
   original-table data unchanged. Use real MySQL, not a mocked SQL execution claim.
   Commands: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/staging-baseline.test.ts`,
   `node tooling/operations/staging-baseline-upgrade.mjs` (read-only default).
3. Checkpoint: only after backup restoration and B2 checks pass, apply the same
   allowlisted additive DDL to the test stand, keep payments disabled and verify
   guest smoke. Record source/target fingerprints and actual execution time.
4. Continue Y4 caller and Y5 scoped payment egress/notification/real-sandbox
   acceptance afterwards. No production, live payment, old migration replay,
   destructive DB reset, new dependencies or unrelated frontend-branch merge.

Code style: portable ESM operational scripts, fixed SQL and generic redacted
failures; e.g. `{ mode: "read-only", state: "pending", releaseApproval: false }`.
Tests belong in `tests/`; execution/rollback evidence belongs in the existing
operations guide. A successful baseline update is not successful payment rollout.

fault fixture; no working table was dropped. Unit/CLI RED→GREEN reaches 47

external-resource skip. No assertion/TTL/timeout weakening or new dependencies.

The exact allowlisted SQL was applied to staging at 2026-10-07T17:17:59Z.
Post-verification: 29 tables, 833 old rows and old definitions unchanged,

### M07 specification draft — background-job lifecycle, 8 October

Status: inspected and specified; M07a local primitive verified below. Executable
integration and shutdown-policy approval are still pending.
This extends the existing workers capability in ADR-005/M07; it is not N12,
another scheduler deployment, a queue service or an ORM rewrite. Review the
shutdown bound before wiring lifecycle changes into the executable.

Objective: give each executable instance explicit ownership of its background
timers and awaited runs, prevent overlapping runs of the same job, and stop
accepting timer work before shutdown. Preserve publication, cancellation/mail,
retention and backup business helpers and the existing cadence. Assumptions:
single executable per application, fixed-interval cadence (not completion-based),
skip an interval while its same job is busy rather than accumulating a backlog.
Different jobs remain independent; local single-flight is not a distributed lock.

Inspection baseline in `_core/index.ts`:

| Job | Initial run | Interval | Existing semantics to preserve |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Scheduled publication | after listener succeeds | 60 seconds | publish SQL first, then announce |
| Billing | after listener succeeds | 30 seconds | cancellation and access-email workers use independent settled promises |
| Maintenance | after listener succeeds | 5 minutes | disabled-shop helper first, then retention |
| Backup due check | after listener succeeds | 6 hours | helper's 24-hour due check and existing activeBackup guard |

All four interval handles are currently discarded. There is no executable
shutdown-hook registration or application-owned stop/drain path. A slow run can
overlap its next scheduled invocation; leases and the backup guard are separate
controls, not proof every job is serial. Publication notifications are detached
with `void`: draining the publication SQL promise alone would not drain those
sends. The database facade does not expose ownership/closing of its mysql2 pool.
These are source inspection findings, not reproduced data loss or paid duplicates.
Local Docker still returns `Error reading remote info: EOF` on 8 October 03:21 UTC;
no disposable database was created and no existing database was accessed.

Implementation order and acceptance (separate local save points):

1. M07a, local scheduling primitive. Keep callbacks/timers explicit and typed;
   constructors have no scheduled effects. Start is idempotent, one immediate
   run per job; interval skips while that job is busy. Sync throw and rejected
   promise release busy state and report only a static job label. Stop fences
   already queued timer callbacks, clears owned handles, is idempotent and
   forbids restarts; it exposes the pending work without pretending it finished.
   Files: `src/workers/interval-jobs.ts`, `tests/worker-lifecycle.test.ts`.
   RED/GREEN: deferred promises and fake timers prove no overlap/backlog, other
   jobs continue, recovery after errors, repeated start/stop and callback fencing.
2. M07b, explicit application-scoped job composition. Nest owns a provider with
   explicit @Inject and no constructor/init autostart. The executable activates
   it only after listen; importing/creating/initializing a factory remains inert.
   Keep the above cadence, helper arguments, disabled-shop gate and transaction
   boundaries. Observe and safely handle database acquisition/run rejection.
   Files: workers module/service, app.module, index and native runtime tests.
   RED/GREEN: controlled dependency boundaries, real Nest factory/listener and
   an isolated built child with synthetic config; failed listen starts no work.
3. M07c, shutdown integration, after the bound is reviewed. Stop/fence timers
   before awaiting owned tasks on app.close and SIGTERM/SIGINT. Never pretend a
   timed-out provider operation failed remotely or mark it sent/cancelled. Keep
   SQL leases recoverable; no forced delete/requeue or automatic payment retry.
   Test graceful completion, unfinished work at the bound, repeated signals,
   no post-stop effects, no unhandled rejection, and actual child exit. Inventory
   detached push work before claiming complete drain; pool ownership/close needs
   its own data-lifecycle slice after in-flight work, not a blind global close.

Use the existing Node 22 / Nest / Vitest / esbuild stack, no new dependency.
Style: small typed services, explicit injection as in TrpcService; examples:
`constructor(@Inject(WorkerJobs) private readonly jobs: WorkerJobs) {}` and
`await jobs.stop()` only when that method's tested drain contract is established.
Paths above are relative to apps/backend except tests in the repository root.
Commands from the root: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/worker-lifecycle.test.ts`
(after the test exists), `pnpm exec vitest run tests/nest-application.test.ts tests/backend-entrypoint.test.ts`, then `pnpm test`, `pnpm check`, `pnpm build`,
`pnpm lint`, `pnpm check:api`, `pnpm check:boundaries`, `pnpm check:docs`.

Always: RED/GREEN each slice, static safe logs, factory-without-jobs invariant,
isolated synthetic transports/config, explicit limits of single-process guards.
Ask first: maximum shutdown wait and handling of tasks still pending at that
bound, any shared-pool ownership change, schema/new dependency or working rollout.
Never: real provider/SMTP/push/S3/SQL work during acceptance, secrets in fixtures,
changing auth/CORS/proxy/HTTP quotas, enabling TEST/shop, or claiming exactly-once.
N12's transactional publication outbox remains separate and open: scheduling
ownership cannot remove the post-commit crash window or provider uncertainty.

This turn is documentation-only. No new runtime behavior or RED/GREEN result is
claimed. Preserve the previously verified M08a commit and all release blockers.
Verification: 50-document local-link check and diff whitespace checks pass;
runtime code is unchanged, so no new application test/build result is claimed.

### M06 signature integrity — own JSON keys, 8 October

Local investigation reproduced a narrow canonicalization defect in
apps/backend/src/prodamus.ts. JSON parsing/spread preserve an own __proto__ key,
but assigning that key into ordinary objects in stringifyValues and sortDeep
does not preserve it as an own property. Consequently an otherwise unchanged
signed JSON callback can acquire that field without invalidating its signature.
The native loopback HTTP reproduction returns 200/ignored before the fix where
400/signature incorrect is expected. No paid-access, settlement or global
Object.prototype pollution exploit is demonstrated by this pending-event test.

Both normalization dictionaries now have a null prototype, retaining every own
JSON key as signature data. Recursive scalar conversion, key sorting, array
order, Unicode, slash escaping, signature keys and comparison are unchanged.
The existing PHP form/query parser intentionally blocks prototype-related path
components; that separate ingress policy is unchanged. This is not a protocol
rewrite, a parser refactor or a claim that Prodamus sends these unusual fields.

Acceptance uses only synthetic keys and independent, hand-derived canonical
strings: ordinary nested data plus root scalar/object/null, nested and array
__proto__ fields; adding an unsigned field must fail verification. The real Nest
HTTP case rejects the tampered callback before settlement. Nine expected RED
assertions were observed; GREEN passes all 90 focused checks across six billing
test files. A deliberate regression of the sorting dictionary alone fails eight
assertions, then restoring it passes again. Input JSON remains unmodified.

unchanged API (73 procedures / 25642 bytes) and 510-file dependency boundaries
pass. Skipped SQL/external suites are not acceptance evidence. Documentation
links, selected-test formatting and diff whitespace are checked before commit.
No dependency, schema, frontend, auth/CORS/proxy/quota, provider/SMTP request,

### M06 safety slice — cancellation transport stays at the configured endpoint

Local native HTTP reproduction on two owned loopback servers confirms that
cancelProdamusSubscription followed 301/302/303/307/308 responses to another
origin and returned true for that destination's `success` body. An unconfigured
same-origin path could also replace the configured cancellation endpoint. All
six expected RED assertions failed on this behavior, not on fixture setup.
This does not demonstrate a compromised merchant or a production incident.

The existing cancellation fetch now uses redirect: error. A redirect is an
unconfirmed attempt, with the existing static warning and false return; it is
not proof of remote cancellation or proof that no remote action happened.

### M07a verified — inert local scheduling primitive, 8 October

IntervalJobs owns one fixed-interval timer and at most one pending callback per
job descriptor. Construction is inert; start schedules one initial microtask
and one interval per job, and repeated start does nothing. A busy interval is
skipped, never queued for catch-up. Jobs remain independent. Internal typed job
names select static failure labels; sync throws and rejected callback promises
are caught without logging exception bodies and release the busy state.
Descriptors are trusted internal configuration: one per job, with positive
Node-supported interval constants, not user input or an environment-driven API.

stop permanently fences future dispatch, clears only owned interval handles,
and returns named promises for pending owned callbacks. It does not await them,
abort work, force completion, restart, impose a shutdown deadline or change any
remote-effect status. Repeated stop exposes whatever is still pending. Callback
settlement includes caught failures; it is not provider-success evidence. A stop
before a queued initial callback enters prevents that callback from running.
Already-entered work stays visible until it settles, including reentrant stop.

Thirteen deferred/fake-timer cases cover inert construction, immediate/fixed
cadence, repeated start/stop, busy skipping/no backlog, independent jobs, both
error forms, unfinished/rejected work, stop-before-start, queued initial/timer
fences, reentrant stop and unrelated timer ownership. Ten expected assertion
failures were observed against the contract-only scaffold. Removing the busy
guard then fails two overlap checks; removing the pre-callback phase fence fails
two stop checks. Restoring both passes 37 focused checks across runner, native
application factory and executable acceptance. Virtual timer cleanup is local;
callbacks only update synthetic counters or controlled deferred promises.

## Prodamus sandbox connection — 8 October, proposed rollout contract

User supplied a time-limited testpage1.payform.ru cabinet and requested test
connection. Inspection found existing subscription definitions and global
ordinary/recurring callback targets, not evidence of another project's ownership.
The user explicitly confirms this test cabinet was issued for DaryaDarcy.
Demo URL creation and opening are verified, not payment completion or application
access. Review the exact notification changes and selected subscription settings;
do not alter existing subscribers or send real recipients during an automated probe.

Official [webhook documentation](https://help.prodamus.ru/payform/tekhnicheskaya-dokumentaciya/webhook-ob-oplate)
distinguishes user-selected demo mode (ordinary signature) from system-disabled
live mode (key with demo suffix). A valid ordinary signature alone is therefore
not a sufficient real-payment boundary. Extend the existing B01 guard first:
top-level demo_mode/demoMode and subscription.demo must prevent entitlement and
cancellation processing, including ambiguous/non-zero markers. Absent, empty,
false and numeric/string zero retain existing live behavior. This guard does not
prove the absence of demo mode when a provider omits the field.

Proposed implementation remains within billing (Nest/TypeScript, existing
Prodamus parser/HMAC/formatter; no SDK or dependency/schema changes in the probe):

1. PD1: repair the existing real-payment demo boundary with failing fixture and
   native HTTP tests. No new test-mode acceptance on the real webhook.
2. PD2, after human review of this contract: add a separate, default-off sandbox
   diagnostic link/receiver. Pin the test merchant and approved application host;
   require dedicated secret configuration. Send only an opaque demo order and
   synthetic contact fixtures. Explicit demo_mode=1 and one-off payment; no card
   saving, subscription, settlement, account creation, mail or SQL writes.
   A signature-valid diagnostic event is not authenticated real-money proof in
   a test cabinet. Browser return is never proof. Do not present receipt
   acknowledgement as access activation or durable payment reconciliation.
3. PD3, after PD2: separately review the exact rollout/rollback and open only the
   required sandbox routes on the test host. Preserve all existing containers,
   overlays, databases and live-payment/shop blocks; no broad outbound access.
   Verify dependency reachability before rollout, receiver delivery with fake
   data, incorrect signature/domain/amount/demo rejection and unchanged access.
4. PD4, after diagnostic/provider evidence: agree isolated test-entitlement
   persistence and user-visible contract before any demo can grant club access.
   Test database replay/rollback only on a disposable, owned database, not the
   working stage DB. Production webhook must still reject demo notifications.
5. Recurring testing requires a verified test subscription with the intended
   price/interval and working notification routing. Existing definitions do not
   imply foreign ownership. An invoice/form alone does not prove renewal delivery.
   Do not rotate keys or modify existing subscribers during connection testing.

Production code belongs in apps/backend/src/billing, behavioral/native tests in
tests, configuration placeholders in .env.example, operational evidence in the
existing staging guide. Match explicit typed boundaries and current formatting;
for example a diagnostic reply is `{ mode: "demo", accessGranted: false }`, never
a successful club checkout reply. Preserve frontend and tRPC contracts initially.

### First email binding — 10 October, approved feature continuation

The human approved admin-only first binding with recipient confirmation, no
merge/replacement and rejection of occupied email. The prior feature-scenario
question is resolved. [Detailed spec](../SPEC-email-binding.md) now records the
API, threat boundaries, ordered slices and acceptance before production code.

New prerequisite found by source inspection: users.email has only a non-unique
lookup index. A read-before-write check cannot prevent concurrent binding and
registration/checkout. Unique normalized identity is required, but existing
upsertUser uses unconditional ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE; adding an email unique
key without guarding cross-identity conflicts can overwrite a different account.
Protect this writer and verify existing callers before enabling the constraint.
This is a proposed schema/concurrency plan, not a tested production defect or
permission to repair old users. Old duplicates must stop rollout, not be merged.

An async human approval question now covers the schema plan, 24-hour confirmation
expiry and separate rollout/mail acceptance. Await that approval before schema
or auth implementation, as required by spec-driven-development/security rules.

socket ping timed out at three seconds; no Docker restart, container/database
changes, secret/config reads, mail dispatch or server access. Original working
branch and the two reviewed admin fixes retained.
